AfD is broadly considered to be a [right-wing](56522.md) and [national-conservative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National-conservative) movement in both [socioeconomic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socioeconomic) and [sociocultural](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sociocultural) terms. AfD's policy brief and mission statement seeks to define the party as both liberal and conservative, with an emphasis on protecting [sovereignty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Sovereignty), [Western identity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_identity), and [German culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_culture) in what it calls a "peaceful, democratic and sovereign [nation-state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nation_state) of the [German people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_people)."[^cite_note-205] Political scientists and journalists have also described AfD as synonymous with [opposition to immigration](6491626.md), [Euroscepticism](10037.md), and holding a [nationalist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nationalist) bent, with various shades of [German nationalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_nationalism) from [civic nationalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civic_nationalism) to hardline sentiments visible in the party. Other commentators have categorized it as a [radical right](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radical_right_\(Europe\)) populist party[^cite_note-206] or as "a typical radical [right-wing populist](8354382.md) party", with an emphasis on [nativism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nativism_\(politics\)).[^cite_note-HeinischMassetti2020-44] Within its elected representation and grassroots membership, AfD has grown to contain interparty factions that range from more moderate conservatives to radicals.[^cite_note-Caiani_&_Císař_2018-207][^cite_note-Gedmin_2019-208]

AfD was initially founded as a [liberal conservative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberal_conservative) party of the [middle class](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle_class),[^cite_note-209] with a tendency towards [soft Euroscepticism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soft_Euroscepticism),[^cite_note-Deutsche_Welle-210] being supportive of Germany's membership in the [European Union](9317.md) but critical of further [European integration](683704.md), the existence of the [euro](9472.md) currency and the bailouts by the [eurozone](184391.md) for countries such as Greece.[^cite_note-CentreRight-211] At that time, the party also advocated support for Swiss-style [semi-direct democracy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Semi-direct_democracy), major reforms to the eurozone, opposition to immigration, and opposed [same-sex marriage](92656.md).[^cite_note-Thompson2015-212][^cite_note-cdn.afd.tools-213] During this period, the party espoused [economic liberal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic_liberal),[^cite_note-Economic_liberal-28] [ordoliberal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ordoliberal),[^cite_note-214] and [national liberal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_liberal) policy stances.[^cite_note-215] Former party MEP [Hans-Olaf Henkel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hans-Olaf_Henkel) likened AfD's early platform to the [Conservative Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conservative_Party_\(UK\)) in Britain rather than [hard Eurosceptic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hard_Eurosceptic) or nationalist parties such as the [UK Independence Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UK_Independence_Party) or the [National Front](67119.md) in France. AfD was also compared to the [Tea Party movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tea_Party_movement) by some media outlets due to its campaigns against eurozone bailouts, although AfD's early leadership disputed this and said it was not looking to attract [right-wing extremists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Right-wing_extremists) into the party.[^cite_note-216]

In 2015 more moderate members, including its founder and former chairman [Bernd Lucke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bernd_Lucke), left AfD after [Frauke Petry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frauke_Petry) was elected chairperson to found a new party, the Alliance for Progress and Renewal, which was renamed the [Liberal Conservative Reformers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberal_Conservative_Reformers) in November 2016.[^cite_note-DW_ALFA_founding-111][^cite_note-BBC_ALFA_founding-112][^cite_note-FAZ_ALFA_renaming-113] When party founder Bernd Lucke had left AfD in 2015, he cited, among other reasons, an "[anti-western](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-Western_sentiment), decidedly [pro-Russian foreign and security policy orientation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AfD_pro-Russia_movement)" as well as increasing calls to "pose the 'system question' with regard to our parliamentary democracy" as reasons for his departure from the party.[^cite_note-\:8-64] At that time, AfD was performing poorly in opinion polls, polling at around 3%, and was suffering infighting; however, an [influx of refugees and migrants](46415102.md) boosted their support later in 2015, with the party turning from matters related to the eurozone to focus on opposing migration, in particular [Muslims](19541.md) and Muslim immigration.[^cite_note-217][^cite_note-218][^cite_note-cdn.afd.tools-213]

AfD underwent a further shift to the right after Petry left the party in 2017 and formed [The Blue Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Blue_Party_\(Germany\)), following AfD's adoption of more hardline [Islamophobic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamophobic), [anti-immigration](6491626.md) positions, and [historical revisionist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Historical_revisionist) remarks by leading AfD figures.[^cite_note-219][^cite_note-220][^cite_note-221] The party now resembles other populist [radical right](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radical_right_\(Europe\)) parties in Europe but is somewhat unusual because it maintains visible ties to even more extreme groups.[^cite_note-arzheimer-2019-222] The party has been described by political scientists as more radical than many other European right-wing populist parties, including the [Sweden Democrats](65873.md), the [Danish People's Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Danish_People's_Party), and the [Freedom Party of Austria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_Party_of_Austria).[^cite_note-223] AfD has been described as, and accused of, containing members sympathetic to the [Identitarian movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Identitarian_movement)[^cite_note-224] and [Pegida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pegida). The AfD leadership has been split on whether to embrace these movements within the party.[^cite_note-225]

In March 2020 the [Federal Office for the Protection of the Constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_Office_for_the_Protection_of_the_Constitution) ([German](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_language): *Bundesamt für Verfassungsschutz*) classified AfD's far-right nationalistic faction known as *[Der Flügel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Der_Fl%C3%BCgel)* as "a right-wing extremist endeavor against the free democratic basic order" and as "not compatible with the Basic Law", placing it under government surveillance.[^cite_note-226][^cite_note-227][^cite_note-Bennhold030321-55] In early March 2021 most of Germany's major media outlets reported that the *Bundesverfassungsschutz* had placed the whole AfD under surveillance as a "suspected extremist group".[^cite_note-BfVBBC-228][^cite_note-BfVDW-229] In response to claims from AfD members that the move was intended to damage the party's chances in the [2021 German federal election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_German_federal_election), the agency stated it would not make public announcements regarding investigations into AfD or its candidates for the foreseeable future.[^cite_note-BfVBBC-228][^cite_note-BfVDW-229] After the revelations, the surveillance was blocked by the courts to give equal opportunities among political parties in a key election year.[^cite_note-230][^cite_note-231][^cite_note-232]

In 2022 it was ruled that the BfV may classify and monitor the entire party as a suspected right-wing extremist group. A corresponding lawsuit by AfD was dismissed because "there were sufficient factual indications of anti-constitutional efforts within the AfD".[^cite_note-Spiegel1-60] The dismissal was upheld in May 2024.[^cite_note-c537-233] On 26 April 2023 the BfV, after four years of investigations into the [Young Alternative for Germany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Young_Alternative_for_Germany), categorized that group as a confirmed extremist organisation. This allowed the chief of the BfV [Thomas Haldenwang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_Haldenwang) to place the youth wing under even more intensive surveillance than the tapping of phone and the use of undercover agents that had been the case until then.[^cite_note-ywtoi-65][^cite_note-reutyw-234] In 2025, the [Federal Office for the Protection of the Constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_Office_for_the_Protection_of_the_Constitution) officially classified AfD as a "confirmed right-wing extremist endeavor,"[^cite_note-\:10-66] which allows authorities to increase surveillance, and permits the use of informants and monitoring of communications.[^cite_note-provenextremist-67] The expertise by the BfV, that led to that classification, was later leaked to the public.[^cite_note-\:14-199][^cite_note-\:13-200]

AfD is [anti-communist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-communist) and engaged in [red-baiting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red-baiting) by comparing [Angela Merkel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angela_Merkel) and her government to [the secret police](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stasi) in [East Germany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Germany).[^cite_note-235]

### Ideological factions

Political commentators and analysts have described the party as containing two prominent factions: subscribers to the more dovish and moderate national-conservative *[Alternative Mitte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alternative_Mitte)* (Alternative Center) wing, such as the parliamentarians [Jörg Meuthen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J%C3%B6rg_Meuthen), [Alice Weidel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alice_Weidel), and [Beatrix von Storch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beatrix_von_Storch), who oppose collaboration with movements or figures like the Pegida founder [Lutz Bachmann](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lutz_Bachmann);[^cite_note-236][^cite_note-237][^cite_note-z232-238] and the more hardline identitarian or [*völkisch* nationalist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/V%C3%B6lkisch_nationalist) *[Der Flügel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Der_Fl%C3%BCgel)* (The Wing) faction, comprising figures at state level such as Thuringia state leader [Björn Höcke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bj%C3%B6rn_H%C3%B6cke).[^cite_note-bbc.com-32][^cite_note-239] Some academics have described additional factions representing [economic liberals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic_liberals)[^cite_note-v974-240][^cite_note-q385-241] or the [Christian right](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_right).[^cite_note-e234-242][^cite_note-HavertzChristian-243][^cite_note-w073-244] The political author [Jeffrey Gedmin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Gedmin) has described the present incarnation of AfD as somewhat lacking in a consistent ideological vision and containing a [broad church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Broad_church) of members who are conservatives, social conservatives, radical-rightists, and others who do not present clear ideological narrative. He also described some of its core voter support as ranging from far-right nationalists to moderate but traditionalist and disaffected conservatives.[^cite_note-Gedmin_2019-208]

### German nationalism

Main article: [Völkisch nationalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/V%C3%B6lkisch_nationalism)

Over time a focus on [German nationalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_nationalism), on reclaiming Germany's sovereignty and national pride, especially in repudiation of [Germany's culture of shame](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_collective_guilt) with regard to its [Nazi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nazism) past, became more central in AfD's ideology and a central plank in its populist appeals.[^cite_note-NYT2017-01-37][^cite_note-Duke-38][^cite_note-Spiegel2016-03-39] Petry, who led the moderate wing of the party, said that Germany should reclaim *völkisch* from its Nazi connotations,[^cite_note-245] while the more right-wing Björn Höcke regularly speaks of the *Vaterland* ("fatherland") and *Volk* ("nation" or "people", but with a strong ethnic or racial connotation).[^cite_note-NYT2017-01-37]

In January 2017 Höcke in a speech stated, in reference to the [Berlin Holocaust Memorial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Memorial_to_the_Murdered_Jews_of_Europe), that "Germans are the only people in the world who plant a monument of shame in the heart of the capital" and criticised this "laughable policy of [coming to terms with the past](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coming_to_terms_with_the_past)".[^cite_note-246][^cite_note-247] Höcke continued that Germany should make a "180 degree" turn with regard to its sense of national pride.[^cite_note-NYT2017-01-37]

#### Accusations of antisemitism

See also: [Antisemitism in 21st-century Germany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antisemitism_in_21st-century_Germany)

Some AfD members and candidates have expressed antisemitic attitudes. AfD leaders have repeatedly denied accusations of [antisemitism](1078.md).[^cite_note-248][^cite_note-249] According to a study conducted by the [Forsa Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forsa_Institute) in 2019, while 2% of the German population [agreed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holocaust_Denial) with the statement that "the [Holocaust](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holocaust) is propaganda of the [Allied Powers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allies_of_World_War_II)", that proportion was 15% among AfD supporters.[^cite_note-250] In 2001, 12 years before the founding of AfD, the former AfD [Bundestag](3768.md) member [Wilhelm von Gottberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wilhelm_von_Gottberg) expressed his views on the [remembrance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Memorialization) of the Holocaust by quoting the Italian [neofascist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neofascist) [Mario Consoli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mario_Consoli?action=edit\&redlink=1) in saying "Any pretext, no matter how flimsy \[...], is good enough to remind people of the Holocaust. The propaganda steamroller is getting stronger rather than weaker over the years, and in more and more countries the Jewish 'truth' about the Holocaust is being given legal protection. The Holocaust must remain a myth, a dogma that is beyond the reach of any free historical research."[^cite_note-251]

In 2017, ten AfD [Bundestag members](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Member_of_the_German_Bundestag) were found to have participated in a closed [Facebook](7529378.md) group named "the Patriots" in which, among other things, [antisemitic](1078.md), [racist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Racism), [pro-Nazi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nazism) and [conspiratorial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_World_Order_\(conspiracy_theory\)) posts were widespread. One [meme](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internet_Meme) posted therein, which showed the Holocaust victim [Anne Frank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anne_Frank)'s face edited on a [pizza box](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pizza_box) labelled with the German equivalent of "fresh from the oven", gained particular media attention.[^cite_note-252][^cite_note-253] While some AfD officials stated that they had been unknowingly added to the Facebook group without consent and that they had now left it, the Bundestag member [Stephan Protschka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephan_Protschka) remained, saying: "I am a member of this group because I also see myself as a patriot."[^cite_note-254][^cite_note-255][^cite_note-256]

According to a 2019 report by the [Federal Office for the Protection of the Constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_Office_for_the_Protection_of_the_Constitution), statements by leading AfD representatives such as [Alexander Gauland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_Gauland) and [Björn Höcke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bj%C3%B6rn_H%C3%B6cke), who exonerate [Holocaust perpetrators](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_major_perpetrators_of_the_Holocaust) and discredit the reappraisal of the [Nazi era](21212.md) as "anti-German", create a "connectivity" to right-wing extremist [historical revisionism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Historical_revisionism) and could "ultimately lead to [denial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holocaust_denial) of [war guilt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Causes_of_World_War_II) and [The Holocaust](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Holocaust)".[^cite_note-257] In 2023 [Felix Klein](https://de.wikipedia.org/wiki/Felix%20Klein%20\(Diplomat\)), the [Federal Government Commissioner for Jewish Life in Germany and the Fight against antisemitism](https://de.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beauftragter%20der%20Bundesregierung%20f%C3%BCr%20j%C3%BCdisches%20Leben%20in%20Deutschland%20und%20den%20Kampf%20gegen%20Antisemitismus), stated that leading forces within AfD relativize the Holocaust and that the party condones [antisemitism](1078.md).[^cite_note-258]

[Josef Schuster](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Josef_Schuster), President of the [Central Council of Jews in Germany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Council_of_Jews_in_Germany), stated in 2024 that he is "concerned that the AfD would deliberately act against Jewish life, if it fits into their concept", and that the party offers [antisemites](1078.md) a home.[^cite_note-259][^cite_note-260] A study commissioned by the [American Jewish Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Jewish_Committee) in 2021 came to the conclusion that [antisemitism](1078.md) belongs to the "programmatic core" of the AfD. According to the study, the party conducts "targeted campaigns" against Jewish celebrities. The study's author, [Lars Rensmann](https://de.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lars%20Rensmann), stated that "despite some lip service to the contrary, hostility towards Israel, Holocaust relativization, antisemitic conspiracy thinking and anti-Jewish images occupy a prominent place" in AfD.[^cite_note-261]

AfD supports [a ban on kosher slaughter within the country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legal_aspects_of_ritual_slaughter), as well as the "import and sale of kosher meat".[^cite_note-262] AfD instead wants to require the stunning of animals before slaughter which is against both Jewish and Muslim religious law that both require animals to be conscious when their necks are cut.[^cite_note-263]

#### Immigration, multiculturalism and Islam

See also: [Immigration to Germany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Immigration_to_Germany)



[Election poster](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Thuringian_state_election) of the [AfD in Thuringia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AfD_Thuringia) supporting [remigration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Remigration) (2024)

AfD describes German [national identity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_identity) as under threat both from European integration and from the presence and accommodation of immigrants and refugees within Germany.[^cite_note-Duke-38][^cite_note-Spiegel2016-03-39] Former leader Petry said in March 2016: "I'm not against immigration, but ... the economic and social consequences of migration on both home and host countries are equally momentous .... The immigration of so many Muslims will change our culture. If this change is desired, it must be the product of a democratic decision supported by a broad majority. But Ms. Merkel simply [opened the borders](46415102.md#Germany_accepts_refugees_stranded_in_Hungary) and invited everybody in, without consulting the parliament or the people."[^cite_note-Spiegel2016-03-39]

In its programme, AfD wants to end what it describes as mass immigration and focus on taking in small numbers of skilled immigrants who are expected to integrate into society and speak German. It encourages German nationals to have more children, as opposed to trying to boost the German population through foreign migration. The party wants to review EU freedom of movement rules and states that immigrants must be employed and contribute to social security through paying taxes for at least four years before being allowed to receive state benefits. AfD calls for [mass deportations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mass_deportation) of foreign born criminals with multiple citizenship or permanent residency. The party describes the Geneva Convention on Refugees as "outdated", calls for stricter vetting of refugees, and believes the German government should invest in special economic and safe zones in third world nations as opposed to taking in large numbers of asylum seekers without background checks.[^cite_note-Wahlprogramm2016-264]

AfD is critical of [multiculturalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multiculturalism) in Germany. The party favours banning the [burqa](165826.md), the Islamic call to prayer in public areas and the construction of new minarets, ending foreign funding of mosques and putting [imams](15240.md) through a state vetting procedure.[^cite_note-bbc.com-32]

AfD began to employ [anti-Muslim rhetoric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-Muslim_rhetoric) during the leadership of Petry, who responded positively to comparisons between the party and Pegida.[^cite_note-bridge-265] In 2016 the party adopted several anti-Muslim positions and stated in its manifesto that "Islam does not belong to Germany. Its expansion and the ever-increasing number of Muslims in the country are viewed by the AfD as a danger to our state, our society, and our values."[^cite_note-bridge-265] The party has run [a billboard campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Slave_Market_\(G%C3%A9r%C3%B4me_painting\)#Use_in_media) that explicitly referenced the far-right [Eurabia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurabia) conspiracy theory,[^cite_note-266] and the party has been seen to have been strongly influenced by,[^cite_note-267] and to be a part of the [counter-jihad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Counter-jihad) movement.[^cite_note-268][^cite_note-othen-269]

In January 2025 the [Karlsruhe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karlsruhe) branch of the AfD initiated a controversial campaign by distributing flyers resembling [flight tickets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Airline_ticket) labeled *"Abschiebetickets"* ("deportation tickets") in mailboxes. These flyers included a QR code linking to AfD Karlsruhe's website and were intended as promotional material for the upcoming Bundestag elections. The action prompted investigations by the criminal police on suspicions of incitement to hatred (*[Volksverhetzung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volksverhetzung)*).[^cite_note-270][^cite_note-271]

In January 2025, after [a deadly knife attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Aschaffenburg_stabbing) perpetrated by an [Afghan](737.md) migrant, who had no [residence permit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Residence_permit), the CDU issued a motion regarding migration into the federal parliament, which attained a majority due to the AfD voting alongside the CDU.[^cite_note-272][^cite_note-273] With this motion, CDU leader [Friedrich Merz](1967626.md) ignored his own proposal, uttered in November 2024, to only put questions to the vote that would find a majority without the AfD.[^cite_note-274] The Bundestag went on to reject the CDU's proposed legislation a few days later, largely due to a dozen CDU legislators abstaining, a decision seen to be sparked by the AfD-related controversy.[^cite_note-275]

### Society

#### LGBT rights



AfD election posters. The poster on the left translates as "German language without [gender neutrality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gender_neutrality_in_languages_with_grammatical_gender#German)"; that on the right as "Only the original".

According to its interim electoral manifesto, AfD opposes [same-sex marriage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_Germany) and favours [civil unions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_union).[^cite_note-Wahlprogramm2016-264] AfD deputy leader [Beatrix von Storch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beatrix_von_Storch) has publicly opposed same-sex marriage. In an effort to overturn same-sex marriage laws, AfD filed a lawsuit over the issue in 2017.[^cite_note-276]

In December 2023, AfD members in Lower Saxony distributed a flier in [Gifhorn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gifhorn_\(district\)) titled "Kindheit unterm Regenbogen" ("Childhood Under the Rainbow"), linking the queer community to pedophilia and genital mutilation, speaking of a "rainbow regime," and claiming that children are being taught to masturbate.[^cite_note-277] They also denounced drag queen story hours, claiming that these "trans activists" "need" children, and that there are only two genders and no "social constructs."[^cite_note-278]

[Hans-Christoph Berndt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hans-Christoph_Berndt), the AfD's [lead candidate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lead_candidate) for the [2024 Brandenburg state election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Brandenburg_state_election), announced that, if elected, he would ban the practice of displaying [rainbow flags](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pride_flag) on public buildings in [the state](3765.md).[^cite_note-279] The AfD parliamentary group of the [Landtag of Lower Saxony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Landtag_of_Lower_Saxony) also introduced a motion to ban rainbow flags from public buildings in 2024.[^cite_note-280]

#### Circumcision

AfD supports a [ban on circumcision for non-medical reasons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Circumcision_and_law) for those under the [age of majority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Age_of_majority), saying that the practice constituted "serious violations of fundamental rights".[^cite_note-281]

#### Abortion

Alternative for Germany is an anti-abortion party.[^cite_note-282][^cite_note-283] The AfD has described its position as "pro-life" and rejects any state support for abortion.[^cite_note-284]

#### Feminism

The left-leaning newspaper *[Die Tageszeitung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Die_Tageszeitung)* described the party as advocating "old gender roles".[^cite_note-285] Wolfgang Gedeon, an elected AfD representative, has included feminism, along with "sexualism" and "migrationism", in an ideology he calls "green communism" that he opposes, and argues for [family values](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Family_values) as part of German identity.[^cite_note-286] As AfD has campaigned for traditional roles for women, it has aligned itself with groups opposed to [modern feminism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Third-wave_feminism).[^cite_note-287] The youth wing of the party has used social media to campaign against aspects of modern feminism, with the support of party leadership.[^cite_note-288]

### Economy

AfD is an [economically liberal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economically_liberal) party.[^cite_note-Economic_liberal-28][^cite_note-289] Despite the 2015 split of economic liberals, AfD can still be broadly characterized as [neoliberal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neoliberal) on economic terms, emphasizing [deregulation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deregulation) and much limited [state intervention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_intervention).[^cite_note-Berger-290] Attempts by some party factions to emphasize [small and medium-sized enterprises](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Small_and_medium-sized_enterprises), and advocate [protectionism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protectionism) over [free trade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_trade), have not had much success.[^cite_note-Caiani_&_Císař_2018-207]

### Environment and climate

AfD has a platform of [climate change denial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_change_denial).[^cite_note-Wahlprogramm2016-264][^cite_note-291][^cite_note-Küppers-7] AfD accepts that the climate is changing, however, it denies that this change is attributable to human influences.[^cite_note-Küppers-7] Instead the party argues that climate change is entirely caused by natural factors. AfD argues that the rising carbon dioxide concentrations have been beneficial (contributed to a "greening" of Earth).[^cite_note-Wahlprogramm2022-292] Next to its climate change denial, AfD opposes far-reaching climate policies: The party opposes energy transformation policies (*[Energiewende](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Energiewende)*), wants to scrap the [German Renewable Energy Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_Renewable_Energy_Act), the German Energy Saving Regulations, and the German Renewable Energy Heat Act. They also want to end bioenergy subsidies and restrict "uncontrolled expansion of wind energy".[^cite_note-Wahlprogramm2016-264] The party rejects the [European Green Deal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Green_Deal) and has warned against the [deindustrialization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deindustrialization) of Europe.[^cite_note-293]

#### Energy

The party argues that the [energy transition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Energy_transition) threatens energy security, possibly leading to energy blackouts. It, therefore, views [lignite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lignite) as the only native energy source that can guarantee German energy security and [energy self-sufficiency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Energy_self-sufficiency).[^cite_note-Küppers-7] Furthermore, AfD wants to reinstate Germany's [nuclear plants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear_plants), arguing that closures between 2002 and 2011 were "economically damaging and not objectively justified". The party argues that the government should "allow a lifetime extension of still operating nuclear power plants on a transitional basis".[^cite_note-Wahlprogramm2016-264] The party opposes amending the [Rome Statute](68762.md) to make [environmental destruction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ecocide) an [international crime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_criminal_law), with [Gunnar Beck](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gunnar_Beck), an MEP for AfD, stating that "recognizing crimes against the environment as a violation of human rights and even war crimes is yet another grotesque inflation of the human rights doctrine."[^cite_note-294]

### Foreign policy

#### Defence

AfD wants a reinstatement of [conscription in Germany](1285284.md), starting for able-bodied men at the age of 18.[^cite_note-FAZ_Wahlprogramm-295][^cite_note-Wahlprogramm2016-264]

#### Russia and the United States

See also: [AfD pro-Russia movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AfD_pro-Russia_movement)

While AfD had been pro-[NATO](21133.md) and [pro-United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlanticism) during the [first Trump administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Trump_administration), it had been sharply critical of the [Biden administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biden_administration).[^cite_note-296] It was significantly divided on whether to support [Russia](25391.md),[^cite_note-Brandt-297] but has since moved to a pro-Russian direction, opposing sanctions on Russia supported by NATO and the United States and calling for an end to military aid to Ukraine.[^cite_note-\:7-54] It is also divided on [free-trade agreements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free-trade_area).[^cite_note-Brandt-297] In March 2019, party leader [Alexander Gauland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_Gauland) said in an interview with the Russian newspaper *[Komsomolskaya Pravda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Komsomolskaya_Pravda)* that they consider the [war in Donbas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_in_Donbas) to be a Ukrainian internal matter, and that Germany should not get involved in the internal affairs of Ukraine or Russia. He also said AfD is against [international sanctions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_sanctions_during_the_Russo-Ukrainian_War) on Russia.[^cite_note-298] AfD members have called for a more independent stance from the United States.[^cite_note-299][^cite_note-300] The party has also endorsed accusations that the United States was involved in the [2022 Nord Stream pipeline sabotage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Nord_Stream_pipeline_sabotage),[^cite_note-301] although by 2026 after investigations they had called for compensation from Ukraine, despite historical denials of involvement by Ukrainian President Zelenskyy.[^cite_note-302][^cite_note-303] AfD has also called NATO's anti-Russian stance overly ideological and detrimental to Germany's interests.[^cite_note-304] A large number of AfD delegates boycotted Ukrainian President [Volodymyr Zelenskyy](59210523.md) in June 2024 when he gave a speech to the [Bundestag](3768.md).[^cite_note-\:7-54] In February 2025, some high-ranking AfD members criticised [Chrupalla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tino_Chrupalla)'s pro-Moscow position on foreign policy.[^cite_note-305]

An [Organized Crime and Corruption Reporting Project](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Organized_Crime_and_Corruption_Reporting_Project) (OCCRP) investigation from February 2023 found evidence that AfD is a key ally for the [International Agency for Current Policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Agency_for_Current_Policy?action=edit\&redlink=1), an organization established by Russian parliamentary staffer [Sargis Mirzakhanian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sargis_Mirzakhanian?action=edit\&redlink=1), whose internal documents describe it as a "closed association of professionals" engaged in a variety of pro-Russian activities.[^cite_note-OCCRP20234-306] These include organizing street [protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protests) against NATO, bringing European delegations to Moscow and Crimea, and targeting "national parliaments of the EU" with resolutions to end [EU sanctions on Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_sanctions_during_the_Russo-Ukrainian_War) and to grant recognition to [Russia's annexation of Crimea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Annexation_of_Crimea_by_the_Russian_Federation). Internal emails, including some from Mirzakhanyan, document substantial payments to EU politicians made in exchange for pro-Kremlin motions in their home countries, and to [Manuel Ochsenreiter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manuel_Ochsenreiter?action=edit\&redlink=1) for publishing pro-Russian propaganda in his *[Zuerst!](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zuerst!)* magazine.[^cite_note-OCCRP20234-306]

In August 2023 a journalist investigation was published by *[The Insider](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Insider_\(website\))*, describing how money was funnelled from Moscow to AfD politicians who initiated a constitutional complaint in Germany against the supplies of weapons for Ukraine.[^cite_note-307] The AfD supported [peace negotiations on the Russo-Ukrainian War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peace_negotiations_in_the_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine) with the participation of Russia.[^cite_note-308]

In October 2025 [Georg Maier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Georg_Maier_\(politician\)), the Interior Minister of [Thuringia](31130.md), raised concerns that the AfD might be using [parliamentary inquiries](16341681.md) to obtain information on [critical infrastructure](521286.md) on behalf of the Kremlin. This includes detailed requests on [transport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transport_infrastructure) and [digital infrastructure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Digital_infrastructure), as well as water and energy supply, military capabilities, weak points and [logistics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_logistics).[^cite_note-309][^cite_note-310] These claims have been supported by other politicians such as [Sonja Eichwede](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sonja_Eichwede), deputy chair of the [SPD](60585.md) parliamentary group who called the AfD a "dangerous role in Putin's geopolitical power game" and [Thomas Röwekamp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_R%C3%B6wekamp), chair of the [Defence Council of the Bundestag](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Defence_Council_of_the_Bundestag?action=edit\&redlink=1) who warned of "critical information about the [Bundeswehr](288188.md) and the [Federal Ministry of Defence](2563857.md) being obtained." The AfD has dismissed these claims with [Björn Höcke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bj%C3%B6rn_H%C3%B6cke), head of the [AfD Thuringia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AfD_Thuringia) calling them "defamation" and [Markus Frohnmaier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Markus_Frohnmaier), [MdB](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Member_of_the_Bundestag) for the AfD, accusing the other parties of creating a "staged outrage".[^cite_note-311]

In March 2026, in the midst of the [United States' war with Iran](82533582.md), AfD MP [Stefan Möller](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stefan_M%C3%B6ller) stated in a meeting of Bundestag members that there were "two hearts in \[AfD's] chest" about [Donald Trump](4848272.md), as "On the one hand", Trump "is pursuing a political approach that is in line with \[AfD's] on many issues, and which also acts as a kind of protective force against attacks on the AfD as an opposition party", but on the other, Trump "is taking this [very aggressive geostrategic course in foreign policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_policy_of_the_second_Trump_administration), which is not in line with \[AfD's] ideas".[^cite_note-312]

#### European Union

AfD initially held a position of [soft Euroscepticism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soft_Euroscepticism) by opposing the euro currency and eurozone bailouts, which the party saw as undermining European integration, but it was otherwise supportive of German membership of the [European Union](9317.md) (EU).[^cite_note-Deutsche_Welle-210]

Since 2015, the party has shifted to a more purely [Eurosceptic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurosceptic) and nationalist position against the EU, calling for the withdrawal from the common European asylum and security policy, significant reform of the EU and a repatriation of powers back from Brussels with some party members endorsing a complete exit from the European Union if these aims are not achievable.[^cite_note-313][^cite_note-314][^cite_note-315][^cite_note-316]

During the 2021 party conference in [Dresden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dresden), a majority of AfD members voted to include more hardline policies against the European Union including German withdrawal from the bloc in the party's manifesto ahead of the [2021 German federal election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_German_federal_election).[^cite_note-317][^cite_note-318][^cite_note-membership-319]

Starting in 2025, the AfD worked with Hungary's nationalist [prime minister](378359.md) at the time, [Viktor Orbán](750582.md). Alice Weidel praised Hungary as a model for AfD, saying that AfD shared Hungary's opposition to illegal immigration and stance on the European Union.[^cite_note-320] In February 2025 Weidel stated about AfD's policy towards the European Union: "We should work together to reform the European Union at all costs. And that can only be done from within. We can achieve this by reducing the [competences of the European Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Competences_of_the_European_Union), by dismantling the entire bureaucratic, expensive—and, in my view, corrupt—superstructure."[^cite_note-Politico120225-321]

#### Middle East

The party had previously been [pro-Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pro-Israel).[^cite_note-timesofisraelantiislam-33][^cite_note-toi2018apr17-322][^cite_note-323] AfD supported the decision of US president [Donald Trump](4848272.md) to recognize [Jerusalem as Israel's capital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_recognition_of_Jerusalem_as_capital_of_Israel), as stated by AfD's [Petr Bystron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petr_Bystron). Despite AfD's pro-Israel stance, Israel has boycotted the party and has refused to hold ties with AfD.[^cite_note-toi2018apr17-322] The party was divided over the [Gaza war](74998519.md), with party leader Chrupalla condemning the [October 7 attacks](74998317.md) but calling for diplomacy and mourning deaths on both sides, while other MPs, such as [Norbert Kleinwächter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norbert_Kleinw%C3%A4chter) and [Rüdiger Lucassen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/R%C3%BCdiger_Lucassen) were critical of Chrupalla's position and openly defended Israel's conduct during the war.[^cite_note-324] Following the October 7 attacks, the party supported cuts in German aid to Palestine via the [UNRWA](32270.md).[^cite_note-325]

In 2024, AfD reversed its previously pro-Israel position, with co-leader [Tino Chrupalla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tino_Chrupalla) calling for an end to Germany's current relationship with Israel, which Chrupalla described as "one-sided", as well as an end to [arms exports to Israel during the Gaza war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_military_aid_to_Israel_during_the_Gaza_war).[^cite_note-rmx-israel-326] This decision drew criticism from some other members of the AfD parliamentary group, suggesting a continued internal divide on the issue.[^cite_note-327] Chrupalla criticised the [German government's support for Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Germany_and_the_Gaza_war) during the war and rejected "blanket" [Islamophobia](162173.md). Some AfD members referred to Israel's conduct as constituting [genocide in Gaza](75668427.md).[^cite_note-rmx-israel-326] Nevertheless, in January 2025, the other co-leader, [Alice Weidel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alice_Weidel), affirmed her support for Israel, but noted that she "didn't know how she would solve this conflict".[^cite_note-328] Chrupalla and Weidel have been described as divided on the issue of Israel, with Weidel continuing to affirm German support for Israel's right to self-defence.[^cite_note-329] At the start of the [Twelve-Day War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twelve-Day_War) between Israel and Iran in June 2025, AfD foreign policy spokesperson [Markus Frohnmaier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Markus_Frohnmaier) expressed support for a diplomatic solution and called on both sides to cease hostilities, while insisting that Iran should not pursue a nuclear weapons programme.[^cite_note-330]

Although the Israeli Foreign Ministry continues to hold no formal contact with AfD,[^cite_note-331] Israeli Diaspora Affairs minister [Amichai Chikli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amichai_Chikli) has praised several of AfD's policy stances and expressed support for Weidel, while criticising [historical revisionist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Historical_revisionist) elements within the party, represented by [Maximilian Krah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maximilian_Krah) and [Björn Höcke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bj%C3%B6rn_H%C3%B6cke).[^cite_note-332][^cite_note-333]

In 2018 and 2019, AfD Bundestag members visited [Ba'athist Syria](57134800.md), meeting with senior officials from the government of [Bashar al-Assad](364813.md) and advocating for the return of Syrian refugees.[^cite_note-334][^cite_note-335]

In 2026, in the midst of the [2026 Iran war](82533582.md), party leader [Tino Chrupalla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tino_Chrupalla) praised [Spain](26667.md)'s refusal to allow American forces to use its military bases for the conflict, and urged Germany to follow Spain's example. He further proposed ending the [stationing of US forces in Germany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_United_States_Army_installations_in_Germany), as well as their [nuclear weapons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Germany_and_weapons_of_mass_destruction) presence in the country.[^cite_note-336]

#### China

AfD views on [China](5405.md) have varied. The party had previously demanded the German government to strip the "developing country" status for China, voicing opposition to "Chinese economic espionage" and opposing Chinese state-owned company [COSCO Shipping](51161421.md) buying of a stake in the [Port of Hamburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Hamburg).[^cite_note-\:0-337]

However, it started changing its position in 2023, with AfD's Bundestag caucus accusing foreign minister [Annalena Baerbock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Annalena_Baerbock) and economic affairs minister [Robert Habeck](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Habeck) of launching an "economic war" against China.[^cite_note-\:0-337] AfD has also criticised restrictions on the use of 5G material from Chinese companies [Huawei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Huawei) and [ZTE](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ZTE). AfD leader [Tino Chrupalla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tino_Chrupalla) has also voiced opposition to restrictions on Chinese technology and backed Chinese foreign minister [Qin Gang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qin_Gang) on his peace-brokering efforts for Russia's invasion of Ukraine.[^cite_note-\:0-337]

AfD currently calls for closer economic, cultural and scientific relations with China.[^cite_note-\:9-338] Nevertheless, in their manifesto for the 2025 federal election, AfD has also called for limits in the involvement of Chinese companies in Germany's seaports and digital infrastructure.[^cite_note-339] It is also opposed to further development aid, including those that would benefit climate change programs or Chinese students in Germany.[^cite_note-\:9-338]

### Party finances

Further information: [Party finance in Germany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Party_finance_in_Germany)

Because the 2013 federal election was the first attempt to join by the party, AfD had not received any federal funds in the run-up to it;[^cite_note-Winand-346] by receiving 2 million votes, it crossed the threshold for party funding and was expected to receive an estimated 1.3 to 1.5 million euros per year of state subsidies.[^cite_note-347] After joining the parliament with more than 90 representatives in the 2017 federal election, the party received more than 70 million euros per year; this probably rose to more than 100 million euros per year from 2019 onward. The party has also established and acknowledged a foundation for political education, and other purposes, close to the party but organized separately, which may be able to claim up to 80 million euros per year.[^cite_note-348] This foundation would need to be acknowledged by the federal parliament in Germany first, but it has a legal claim to these subsidies.

In 2018 the [Alternative for Germany donation scandal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alternative_for_Germany_donation_scandal) became public, as federal and European Parliament politicians Alice Weidel, Jörg Meuthen, Marcus Pretzell, and [Guido Reil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guido_Reil) had profited from illegal and unnamed donations from non-EU countries. The acceptance of donations from non-EU countries is prohibited for German parties and politicians.

### Young Alternative for Germany

Main article: [Young Alternative for Germany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Young_Alternative_for_Germany)

Young Alternative for Germany ([German](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_language): *Junge Alternative für Deutschland*, JA) was founded in 2013 as the youth organisation of AfD, while remaining legally independent from its mother party.[^cite_note-Bernd_Lucke_und_die_wilde_Jugend-79] In view of JA's independence, it has been regarded by some in AfD's hierarchy as being somewhat wayward,[^cite_note-Lamparski-349] with JA repeatedly accused of being "too far-right",[^cite_note-Krass-350] politically regressive and [antifeminist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antifeminist) by the German mainstream media.[^cite_note-Lamparski-349][^cite_note-Whitefem-351][^cite_note-352]

In December 2024 the main party of the AfD announced its intention to cut ties with the JA in connection to its classification by the Federal Office for the Protection of the Constitution. AfD leadership are planning to found a new organisation as its youth wing.[^cite_note-353] On 12 January 2025, AfD leadership voted to formally replace the JA as its youth organisation.[^cite_note-354]

### International affiliation and relations

Following the [2014 European Parliament election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_European_Parliament_election) on 12 June, AfD was accepted into the [European Conservatives and Reformists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Conservatives_and_Reformists_Group) (ECR) group in the [European Parliament](9581.md).[^cite_note-Nicolaou-100] In February 2016, AfD announced a closer cooperation with the right-wing populist party [Freedom Party of Austria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_Party_of_Austria) (FPÖ), which was a member of the [Europe of Nations and Freedom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Europe_of_Nations_and_Freedom) (ENF) group.[^cite_note-afdfpoe-115] On 8 March 2016, the bureau of the ECR group began motions to exclude AfD MEPs from their group due to the party's links with the far-right FPÖ and controversial remarks by two party leaders about shooting immigrants.[^cite_note-Euractiv1-116][^cite_note-EUobserver_March_2016-117] MEP Beatrix von Storch pre-empted her imminent expulsion by leaving the ECR group to join the [Europe of Freedom and Direct Democracy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Europe_of_Freedom_and_Direct_Democracy) group on 8 April,[^cite_note-Storch_quits_ECR-118][^cite_note-Storch_joins_EFDD-119] and Marcus Pretzell was expelled from the ECR group on 12 April 2016.[^cite_note-Pretzell_expelled-120] During the party convention on 30 April 2016, Pretzell announced his intention to join the Europe of Nations and Freedom group,[^cite_note-NatFront-355][^cite_note-356] although he subsequently left AfD to join Petry's [Blue Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blue_Party_\(Germany\)).[^cite_note-357]

In April 2019, [Jörg Meuthen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J%C3%B6rg_Meuthen) appeared alongside [Northern League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_League_\(Italy\)) leader [Matteo Salvini](1767165.md), [National Rally](67119.md) leader [Marine Le Pen](572897.md), and politicians from the [Danish People's Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Danish_People's_Party) and FPÖ to announce the formation of a new European political alliance.[^cite_note-358] AfD later joined this group in the European Parliament, which was ultimately named the [Identity and Democracy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Identity_and_Democracy) group.[^cite_note-359] In July 2023, the AfD joined the [Identity and Democracy Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Identity_and_Democracy_Party), the [European political party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_political_party) affiliated with this group.[^cite_note-360]

The AfD initially maintained close cooperation with the French [National Rally](67119.md) and Marine Le Pen. In February 2024, it was reported that the relations between the two parties had become strained after AfD spokesmen attended the [2023 Potsdam far-right meeting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Potsdam_far-right_meeting). In response, the AfD's leadership held a meeting with Le Pen and denied endorsing the words of some of the people at the meeting.[^cite_note-361][^cite_note-362]

In May 2024, it was reported that the National Rally and other members of the Identity and Democracy group had announced they would no longer sit with the AfD following the [2024 European Parliament election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_European_Parliament_election) after AfD's lead candidate for the election [Maximilian Krah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maximilian_Krah) made remarks in an interview on Nazi Germany and allegedly suggested that not all members of the [Waffen-SS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Waffen-SS) should be seen as criminals.[^cite_note-363][^cite_note-364] Italy's [Lega](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lega_\(political_party\)) and the Czech [Freedom and Direct Democracy](46867556.md) (SPD) backed the National Rally's decision and announced they would also formally cease cooperation with the AfD while the [Danish People's Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Danish_People's_Party) issued an ultimatum that they would only continue working with AfD on the condition of Krah's removal. The Flemish [Vlaams Belang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vlaams_Belang) criticised Krah's words as "increasingly problematic" but declined to immediately expel the AfD faction, stating they preferred to review the situation after the election. The Estonian [EKRE](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EKRE) and the [FPÖ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FP%C3%96) supported expelling Krah but opposed the expulsion of AfD. After an internal meeting and vote, the Identity and Democracy board subsequently agreed to expel AfD, with group leader [Marco Zanni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marco_Zanni) citing Krah's interview, as well as allegations of Chinese and Russian espionage influence on AfD. The party consequently moved to [non-inscrits](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-inscrits).[^cite_note-365][^cite_note-366][^cite_note-367] Following the decision, AfD said they would negotiate to rejoin the group and announced Krah would not sit with the AfD faction in the European Parliament after the election.[^cite_note-euronews.com-179]

AfD also has ties to parties like Hungary's [Our Homeland Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Our_Homeland_Movement),[^cite_note-368] the [Confederation of the Polish Crown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confederation_of_the_Polish_Crown) (KKP),[^cite_note-369] the Dutch [Forum for Democracy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forum_for_Democracy) (FvD),[^cite_note-370] the French [Reconquête](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reconqu%C3%AAte),[^cite_note-371] Slovakia's [Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_\(Slovakia\)) party,[^cite_note-372] the [Liberal Democratic Party of Belarus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberal_Democratic_Party_of_Belarus),[^cite_note-373] Bulgaria's [Revival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Revival_\(Bulgarian_political_party\)),[^cite_note-374] [Alternative for Sweden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alternative_for_Sweden),[^cite_note-375] Serbia's [Dveri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dveri)[^cite_note-376] and the [Serbian Party Oathkeepers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serbian_Party_Oathkeepers).[^cite_note-377] Following the [2024 European Parliament election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_European_Parliament_election), the AfD began negotiations with several of these parties to form a new European Parliament group.[^cite_note-378] Although the AfD originally negotiated with [S.O.S. Romania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S.O.S._Romania),[^cite_note-379][^cite_note-380] it later rejected S.O.S.'s attempt to join the group.[^cite_note-381] The KKP was also eventually rejected from the group.[^cite_note-382] Despite the Czech SPD's prior declaration that it would not sit with the AfD, and past opposition by Poland's [New Hope](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Hope_\(Poland\)) to cooperation with the AfD, both parties ultimately joined the AfD-led [Europe of Sovereign Nations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Europe_of_Sovereign_Nations_Group) group.[^cite_note-383][^cite_note-384][^cite_note-385] In August 2024, AfD also formed the [Europe of Sovereign Nations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Europe_of_Sovereign_Nations_\(party\)) party.[^cite_note-386]

In the United States, AfD has connections with former [Department of Government Efficiency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Department_of_Government_Efficiency) leader [Elon Musk](909036.md)[^cite_note-387] and groups associated with the [Republican Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republican_Party_\(U.S.\)), particularly the [Young Republicans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Young_Republicans).[^cite_note-388][^cite_note-389] In response to the German domestic intelligence agency 2025 classification of the party as a "confirmed right-wing extremist endeavor" permitting monitoring of it, US Vice President [JD Vance](53396477.md) wrote, "The West tore down the Berlin Wall together. And it has been rebuilt – not by the Soviets or the Russians, but by the German establishment." US secretary of state [Marco Rubio](5502549.md) also wrote "Germany just gave its spy agency new powers to surveil the opposition. That's not democracy – it's tyranny in disguise."[^cite_note-telegraph-20250503-390]

AfD has contacts with the [People's Party of Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People's_Party_of_Canada) through the AfD MEP [Christine Anderson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christine_Anderson).[^cite_note-391] The former [President of Switzerland](33730924.md), [Ueli Maurer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ueli_Maurer) of the [Swiss People's Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swiss_People's_Party), has endorsed the 2025 AfD election campaign and addressed AfD events.[^cite_note-392]

In February 2025, Hungarian Prime Minister [Viktor Orbán](750582.md) and Weidel announced the start of cooperation between AfD and [Fidesz](404647.md).[^cite_note-Politico120225-321] During its [2025 German federal election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_German_federal_election) campaign, the AfD also received support from the Spanish [Vox](41928488.md) party[^cite_note-393] and the [Alliance of Independent Social Democrats](4358875.md) in [Bosnia and Herzegovina](3463.md).[^cite_note-394]

AfD has some links with the UK [Homeland Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homeland_Party_\(UK\)).[^cite_note-395]

## Public profile

### Early days



AfD leaders in 2013

At the outset, AfD presented itself as [conservative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conservative) and [middle-class](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle_class), catering to a well-educated demographic; around two-thirds of supporters listed on its website in the early days held doctorates, leading to AfD being nicknamed the "professors' party" in its early days.[^cite_note-Wittrock-396][^cite_note-NYT-397][^cite_note-Connolly-398] The party was described as professors and academics who dislike the compromises inflicted on their purist theories by German party politics.[^cite_note-Scally-399] 86% of the party's initial supporters were male.[^cite_note-Paulick-89]

### Relationship with other groups



Sticker of [nationalistic Identitarian movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Identitarian_movement) at AfD Bavaria Banner

It was alleged that outside the Berlin hotel where the party held its inaugural meeting, copies of *[Junge Freiheit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Junge_Freiheit)*, a weekly that is also popular with the far right, were being handed out.[^cite_note-Barkin-400] The *[Rheinische Post](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rheinische_Post)* pointed out that some AfD members and supporters wrote for the paper.[^cite_note-Weinthal-82][^cite_note-401] There was also a protest outside the venue of the party's inaugural meeting by Andreas Storr, a [National Democratic Party of Germany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Democratic_Party_of_Germany) (NPD) representative in the [Landtag of Saxony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Landtag_of_the_Free_State_of_Saxony), as the NPD sees AfD as a rival for Eurosceptic votes.[^cite_note-402]

In 2013, AfD party organizers sent out the message that they are not trying to attract right-wing radicals and toned down rhetoric on their [Facebook](7529378.md) page following media allegations that it too closely evoked the language of the far right.[^cite_note-Wittrock-396][^cite_note-403] At that time, AfD checked applicants for membership to exclude [far-right](221220.md) and former NPD members who support the anti-euro policy.[^cite_note-Wittrock-396][^cite_note-NYT-397][^cite_note-Alexander-404] The former party chairman Bernd Lucke stated that "\[t]he applause is coming from the wrong side", regarding praise his party gained from the NPD.[^cite_note-Wittrock-396]

Members of [Alliance 90/Green Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alliance_90/The_Greens) have accused AfD of pandering to xenophobic and nationalistic sentiments.[^cite_note-Heine-405] There have been altercations between AfD members and [Green Youth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Green_Youth_\(Germany\)) members.[^cite_note-Heine-405] Following the 2013 federal election, the anti-Islam [German Freedom Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_Freedom_Party) unilaterally pledged to support AfD in the 2014 elections and concentrate its efforts on local elections only.[^cite_note-Stadke-406] Bernd Lucke responded by saying that the German Freedom Party's support was unwanted and sent a letter to AfD party associations recommending a hiring freeze.[^cite_note-Leber-407]

*[Stern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stern_\(magazine\))* reported that among 396 AfD candidates for the 2017 Bundestag, 47 candidates did not distance themselves from right-wing extremism. Although a large proportion of the candidates are not openly racist, some relativize Germany's role in World War II or call for the recognition of a "Cult of Guilt". 30 candidates claimed to tolerate right-wing friends in their profile or were themselves members of groups associated with such people; others said that they mourned the [German Reich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_Reich) or used their symbols.[^cite_note-408]

In 2018 [Tino Chrupalla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tino_Chrupalla), the current co-leader of AfD, gave an interview to [holocaust denier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holocaust_denial), [antisemite](1078.md) and [right-wing extremist](221220.md) [Nikolai Nerling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nikolai_Nerling), which was uploaded to [YouTube](3524766.md). It was staged as having occurred by chance, but an earlier shot in the video reveals Chrupalla waiting in the background. As such, the interview was cited in the 2019 [Federal Office for the Protection of the Constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_Office_for_the_Protection_of_the_Constitution) report on AfD as evidence of the party's "Connections to the framework of a so-called new right or right-wing populist 'resistance milieu'".[^cite_note-409][^cite_note-410]

On 24 June 2024 it was announced that two parliamentary groups consisting of members of the AfD and [Die Heimat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Democratic_Party_of_Germany) (formerly the NPD) had been formed in the [Brandenburg](3765.md) town of [Lauchhammer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lauchhammer) and the district of [Oberspreewald-Lausitz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oberspreewald-Lausitz). In Lauchhammer, the joint parliamentary group will be represented in the town council under the name "AfDplus", while the "Heimat & Zukunft" parliamentary group has been formed in the district council of Oberspreewald-Lausitz. Thomas Gürtler from *Die Heimat* will play a leading role in both bodies. This development is seen as the first official coalition between AfD and the far-right party *Die Heimat*. The formation of the parliamentary groups was supported by statements made by AfD chairman [Tino Chrupalla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tino_Chrupalla), who emphasised that there would be no "firewalls" to other parties at local level.[^cite_note-411]

### Refugees

In 2016 AfD MEP [Marcus Pretzell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marcus_Pretzell) was expelled from the party after he said that German borders should be defended from incursion by refugees "with armed force as a measure of last resort".[^cite_note-Pretzell_expelled-120]

Later that same year, former AfD [party chair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Party_chair) and MEP [Frauke Petry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frauke_Petry) told a reporter from the regional newspaper *Mannheimer Morgen* that the German Border police must do their jobs by "hindering illegal entry of refugees" and that they may "use firearms if necessary" to "prevent illegal border crossings".[^cite_note-IndepShoot-412][^cite_note-mannheimerjanuary2016-413] Petry later stated that no policeman "wants to fire on a refugee and I don't want that either" but that border police must follow the law to maintain the integrity of European borders. Afterwards, Petry made several attempts to justify these statements.[^cite_note-mannheimerjanuary2016-413]

### Pegida



[Alice Weidel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alice_Weidel) and [Alexander Gauland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_Gauland) in April 2017

In response to the [Pegida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pegida) movement and demonstrations, members of AfD have expressed different opinions of it, with Lucke describing the movement as "a sign that these people do not feel their concerns are understood by politicians".[^cite_note-Huggler14-414] In response to the CDU Interior Minister [Thomas de Maizière](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_de_Maizi%C3%A8re) alleging an "overlap" between Pegida rallies and AfD, Alexander Gauland stated that AfD are "natural allies of this movement".[^cite_note-415] Hans-Olaf Henkel asked members of the party not to join the demonstrations, telling *[Der Tagesspiegel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Der_Tagesspiegel)* that he believed it could not be ruled out that they had "xenophobic or even racist connotations".[^cite_note-Huggler14-414]

A straw poll by *[The Economist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Economist)* found that nine out of ten Pegida protesters would back the AfD.[^cite_note-416]

### Neo-Nazi controversies



[Björn Höcke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bj%C3%B6rn_H%C3%B6cke) at a rally for the 2019 state election

In January 2017 [Björn Höcke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bj%C3%B6rn_H%C3%B6cke), one of the founders of AfD,[^cite_note-417][^cite_note-418][^cite_note-419][^cite_note-420] gave a speech in [Dresden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dresden) in which, referring to the [Memorial to the Murdered Jews of Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Memorial_to_the_Murdered_Jews_of_Europe), he stated that "we Germans are the only people in the world who have planted a memorial of shame in the heart of their capital",[^cite_note-FAZ-2017-421] and suggested that Germans "need to make a 180 degree change in their politics of commemoration".[^cite_note-422] The speech was widely criticised as [antisemitic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antisemitism_in_21st-century_Germany) or [neo-Nazi](54361.md), among others by Jewish leaders in Germany.[^cite_note-FAZ-2017-421][^cite_note-423] Within AfD, he was described by his party chairwoman, [Frauke Petry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frauke_Petry), as a "burden to the party", while other members of the party, such as [Alexander Gauland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_Gauland), said that they found no antisemitism in the speech.[^cite_note-FAZ-2017-421]

In February 2017, AfD leaders asked for Höcke to be expelled from the party due to his speech. The arbitration committee of AfD in [Thuringia](31130.md) was set to rule on the leaders' request.[^cite_note-424] In May 2018, an AfD tribunal ruled that Höcke was allowed to stay in the party.[^cite_note-425]

The AfD politician [Matthias Helferich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matthias_Helferich) had described himself in social media messages as the "friendly face of the ns \[*[sic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sic)*]," referring to [National Socialism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naziism). Helferich also called himself a "democratic [Freisler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roland_Freisler)," referring to a Nazi-era judge.[^cite_note-426]

In January 2024 it was revealed that senior members of the party, including [Roland Hartwig](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roland_Hartwig), then advisor to party co-leader [Alice Weidel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alice_Weidel), attended a [meeting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Potsdam_far-right_meeting) alongside neo-Nazi influencers, where plans for the deportation of millions of "asylum seekers", "non-assimilated people", and those with "non-German backgrounds" were discussed, including those with German citizenship and residency rights.[^cite_note-427] The event triggered the [2024 German anti-extremism protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_German_anti-extremism_protests).

In May 2024, Höcke was convicted and fined €13,000 by the state court in [Halle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Halle_\(Saale\)) for deliberately using a banned slogan *"Alles für Deutschland"*, associated with the Nazi Party's [paramilitary wing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sturmabteilung), in a May 2021 campaign speech.[^cite_note-428] In the same month, the party member [Maximilian Krah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maximilian_Krah) defence of the *[Waffen-SS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Waffen-SS)* caused controversy.[^cite_note-429][^cite_note-430]

Following the [2025 federal election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_German_federal_election) it was confirmed that Maximillian Krah and Matthias Helferich would be joining the parliament.[^cite_note-431][^cite_note-432]

### 'Extremist' designation

On 2 May 2025 the German domestic intelligence agency, [Bundesamt für Verfassungsschutz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bundesamt_f%C3%BCr_Verfassungsschutz) (BfV), published a report spanning 1,100 pages which sought to assess whether the AfD should be designated as an 'extremist' group. Central to its thesis was the party's anti-Muslim stance, and it favouring national and ethnic Germans over migrants. AfD denounced its designation as a politically motivated attempt to discredit and criminalize it. The designation opens the possibility of civil servants belonging to the AfD facing dismissal depending on their role within the entity, according to the country's interior ministry.[^cite_note-Reuters020525-188] In response, the AfD filed a lawsuit.[^cite_note-433]

### Alleged Pro-Russia movement

Main article: [AfD pro-Russia movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AfD_pro-Russia_movement)

The German [domestic secret service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_Office_for_the_Protection_of_the_Constitution) reported based on its findings that Russia is trying to "destabilize the democratic system of Germany on many levels." According to the head of the service [Thomas Haldenwang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_Haldenwang), Russian narratives are being spread by parts of the AfD and are contributing to the expansion of right-wing populism.[^cite_note-434][^cite_note-435]

AfD members and activists were listed as keeping close ties with Russian politicians and receiving financial benefits in an [OCCRP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/OCCRP) investigation of Russia's International Agency for Current Policy.[^cite_note-OCCRP20234-306]

### Initiatives and Networks

#### Vadar

The AfD Politicians [Eugen Schmidt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eugen_Schmidt_\(politician\)), [Olga Petersen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olga_Petersen), and [Ulrich Oehme](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ulrich_Oehme) founded the "Association for the Prevention of Discrimination and Exclusion of Russian-Germans and Russian-speaking Fellow Citizens in Germany" (Vadar e.V.) in June 2022.[^cite_note-\:42-436] The association attests that Germany has an "anti-Russian mood" and wants to offer legal help to "Russian-Germans and Russian-speaking fellow citizens" who would be discriminated against or excluded by the war of aggression.[^cite_note-\:02-437] The association says it financed the legal representation of pro-Russian influencer [Alina Lipp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alina_Lipp) and is opposed to the German ban on the [Russian military and propaganda symbol *Z*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Z_\(military_symbol\)).[^cite_note-\:42-436][^cite_note-\:52-438] The association denies [Russian war crimes in the Russo-Ukrainian War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_crimes_in_the_Russo-Ukrainian_War).[^cite_note-439][^cite_note-440]

According to a report in the *[Sächsische Zeitung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A4chsische_Zeitung)*, Vadar shares a bank account with an institution that is majority Russian-owned. According to German public broadcaster ARD, German security authorities are investigating the association connections to Russian authorities.[^cite_note-\:02-437]

#### International Agency for Current Policy

An [Organized Crime and Corruption Reporting Project](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Organized_Crime_and_Corruption_Reporting_Project) (OCCRP) investigation from February 2023 found evidence that AfD is a key ally for the International Agency for Current Policy, an organization established by Russian parliamentary staffer Sargis Mirzakhanian whose internal documents describe it as a "closed association of professionals" engaged in a variety of pro-Russian activities.[^cite_note-OCCRP20234-306] These include organizing street [protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protests) against NATO, bringing European delegations to Moscow and Crimea, and targeting "national parliaments of the EU" with resolutions to end [EU sanctions on Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_sanctions_during_the_Russo-Ukrainian_War) and to grant recognition to [Russia's annexation of Crimea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Annexation_of_Crimea_by_the_Russian_Federation). Internal emails, including some from Mirzakhanyan, document substantial payments to EU politicians made in exchange for pro-Kremlin motions in their home countries, and to journalist and AfD member [Manuel Ochsenreiter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manuel_Ochsenreiter?action=edit\&redlink=1) for publishing pro-Russian propaganda in his *[Zuerst!](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zuerst!)* magazine.[^cite_note-OCCRP20234-306]

[^cite_note-:18-12]: &#x20;Salzborn, Samuel (2019). "Antisemitism in the 'Alternative for Germany' Party". In Langenbacher, Eric (ed.). *Twilight of the Merkel Era: Power and Politics in Germany after the 2017 Bundestag Election*. New York: Berghahn. p. 254. [ISBN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ISBN_\(identifier\)) [978-1-78920-264-9](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special\:BookSources/978-1-78920-264-9). The relatively new party known as the Alternative for Germany (Alternative für Deutschland, AfD) and its relationship to right-wing extremism have been the subject of a great deal of intensive discussion among political and social scientists. While one stream of research focuses primarily on the strategic aspects of the AfD, such as its populist rhetoric and use of social media, another devotes more attention to the worldview of the AfD, and its increasing radicalization from a right-wing conservative party to a right-wing extremist one... It has become undeniable that the AfD has now adopted large parts of the far-right tradition, including racism and völkisch nationalism (a form of ethnonationalism) as central components with an ideology of inequality, alongside nationalist protectionism and anti-EU economic positions, an emphatic rejection of parliamentarianism and representative democracy, and a long-standing antifeminism and hostility towards gender equality.

Within only a few years the party changed its ideological position significantly and moved gradually but rapidly further to the far-right fringe, with a combination of populism, authoritarianism, volkish nationalism, and xenophobia as the central elements of their ideology.

Yet this camouflages the AfD's true ideology – it is much more than classic populism that sets the 'pure people against the corrupt elite'. Even if seen in populism's triage – ideology, style, and strategy – the AfD goes well beyond that...

[^cite_note-:272-17]: &#x20;Fitzi, Gregor; Mackert, Jürgen; Turner, Bryan Stanley (2019). *Populism and the crisis of democracy*. Routledge advances in sociology. Why should we denote Germany's AfD... as right-wing populist? Too often it seems that 'populism' operates \[inaccurately] as a kind of euphemism for (neo-)fascism.

The AfD ran a politically savvy campaign. It tapped into historical grievances in former communist eastern Germany, by co-opting phrases from the dissident movement that brought down the Berlin Wall 30 years ago. The AfD posters demanded a 'Wende 2.0', using the German word for the peaceful revolution that brought down East German communism, and the AfD leaders compared Mrs Merkel's government to the Stasi secret police.

There are essentially three ideological currents fighting for supremacy within the right-wing project as a whole and the AfD in particular. Firstly, there is the national-conservative line, which includes the weekly newspaper Junge Freiheit, anti-feminist networks and Christian fundamentalists ... Georg Paszderski and Beatrix von Storch are two prominent figures within the AfD who belong to the national-conservative current. The second ideological current are the national-neoliberals, who played an important role in the founding phase of the AfD. However, even after the departure of initiators such as economics professor Bernd Lucke and former head of the Federation of German Industries Hans-Olaf Henkel, as well as long- time party leader Jörg Meuthen, the AfD's substantive orientation is largely characterized by neo- or ordoliberal positions. Notable figures within the AfD, including parliamentary group leader and party chairwoman Alice Weidel and deputy chairman Peter Boehringer, can be attributed to the national-neoliberal current.
